  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Washington, leader of America (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: America
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Minuteman, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Monitor, Replacing Ironclad (Requires technology: Industrialization)
        - Unique Building: Independence Hall, Replacing Royal Collection (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Building: Homestead, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Civilization Ability: Military Land Units start with the Sentry Promotion. Tile purchase cost is reduced by 25% and is reset upon founding a City. +20 Production when purchasing tiles, scaling with Era. Can purchase tiles owned by others.
      - Leader: Washington
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 6
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 4
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 5
        - Boldness: 4
        - WarBias: 4
        - HostileBias: 7
        - WarmongerHate: 8
        - NeutralBias: 4
        - FriendlyBias: 9
        - GuardedBias: 3
        - AfraidBias: 5
        - DiplomaticBalance: 8
        - Friendliness: 9
        - WorkWithWillingness: 9
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 5
        - Loyalty: 7
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 9
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 3
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 2
        - DenounceWillingness: 2
        - Forgiveness: 2
        - Meanness: 3
        - Neediness: 7
        - Chattiness: 1
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (Reducing Boldness from 5 to 4 and WarBias from 5 to 4 in response to India's peace treaty expiring with their overwhelming military advantage. We must be more cautious and avoid provoking additional conflicts until we can strengthen our forces. VictoryCompetitiveness reduced from 7 to 6 - survival takes priority over competitive victory pursuit right now. DiplomaticBalance reduced from 8 to 7 as defensive preparations become more critical. WorkWithWillingness increased to 9 from 8 - we need maximum cooperation from allies Netherlands and Polynesia. HostileBias reduced from 8 to 7 to avoid escalation. Friendliness remains 9 to maintain critical alliances. Meanness at 3, not aggressive given our military disadvantage. Chattiness at 1 to avoid drawing unwanted attention.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: Selecting Nuclear Fission (unlocks Atomic Bombs, Nuclear Plants) after Rocketry completes. India's overwhelming military superiority (4.5x our strength) makes nuclear deterrence our only viable survival option. Atomic Bombs are essential as equalizers against conventional forces. Nuclear Plants boost production for military units and spaceship components. However, this research is dependent on acquiring Uranium (currently 0) through diplomacy with Netherlands (4 Uranium) or Portugal (2 Uranium). Researching Nuclear Fission first preserves Nuclear Fission-specific buildings while we work on Uranium acquisition.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Elite Forces (Policy)
        - Rationale: Selecting Elite Forces (Level 1 Autocracy Tenet) as next policy. Provides +15 Experience for newly created Military Units and 50% more Experience from combat. This is invaluable for building stronger defensive forces quickly against India's overwhelming military. The experience bonuses help our units gain promotions faster, improving combat effectiveness and survival rates despite numerical disadvantage. With America fighting Poland (captured San Francisco), Persia (ongoing war), and now obligated to defend against India, having higher-quality units with better promotions becomes critical for successful defense. Elite Forces synergizes with our military-focused Autocracy ideology and supports desperate current wars.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### America
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Maintaining alliance with Polynesia unchanged. Polynesia remains America's strongest ally despite being Unhappy (48%). Their 8 World Congress delegates are crucial, and they continue fighting Persia (War Score: 24% in their favor). Positive relationship ensures diplomatic support in the upcoming World Congress vote (Treasure Fleet proposal by Polynesia). Trade relationships and open borders remain active. No change from current strong baseline.
        - UpdatedTurn: 213
        
        ### Portugal
        - Public: 5
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Maintaining positive relationship with Portugal (unchanged at 5/5). Portugal is an Industrial-era power with 7 delegates, strong military (1509 strength), and extensive trade network. Their defensive pact with India and DoF with The Netherlands makes them geopolitically significant. The modest positive signal maintains diplomatic stability while avoiding overcommitment to a potential rival. Current relationship supports peaceful coexistence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 264
        
        ### Valletta
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Strong negative relationship toward Valletta after their declaration of war on America and Egypt. This city-state allied with India and now attacks both us and our ally. Setting Public -50 and Private -30 signals our strong hostility and guides our AI to prioritize Valletta as a target. This aggressive modifier helps coordinate military response against the new threat while ensuring we don't waste diplomatic resources on reconciliation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 311
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 25
        - Rationale: Strengthening relationship with Egypt (Private from 20 to 25, Public unchanged at 20). Egypt is our key defensive pact ally fighting multiple fronts. Despite losing Alexandria to India and being Unhappy, Egypt remains committed. India has proposed sanctioning Egypt in World Congress. Strengthening this relationship signals support and encourages Egypt to maintain pressure on Poland and India. Egypt's military is weak (687) so diplomatic solidarity is crucial.
        - UpdatedTurn: 315
        
        ### Ormus
        - Public: -10
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Setting negative relationship with Ormus after their declaration of war on America and Egypt. While Ormus is a minor city-state (no strategic value), this signals our resolve to respond to aggression and guides AI to prioritize Ormus as a hostile actor in the ongoing wars against India and their allies. The moderate negative values reflect that this is a minor threat compared to Poland/India.
        - UpdatedTurn: 331
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Significantly worsening relationship with Poland after their capture of San Francisco (Smithsonian Institution wonder lost, valuable Oil/Aluminum resources taken, 14 population city captured). Poland's Winged Hussars and Landships continue aggressive operations. Public penalty increased to -40 and Private to -30 signals our determination to recover the city and respond militarily. This hostile modifier will guide AI for sustained conflict until San Francisco is recaptured or adequate compensation achieved. The relationship deterioration reflects the severity of this territorial loss and Poland's escalating warmongering behavior (now controlling both Thebes and San Francisco).
        - UpdatedTurn: 340
        
        ### Persia
        - Public: -35
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: Worsening relationship with Persia after Ergili's capture. While we're winning this war (War Score: 42%), Persia still controls valuable territory and potentially holds antigues/resources. More hostile stance (Public -30→-35, Private -20→-25) signals our intent to continue pressing military advantage while Persia remains weakened. Persia attacked unprovoked with Poland and India, and this stronger hostile modifier helps AI maintain aggressive posture toward Persia for potential additional gains before peace negotiations. Persia is now the weakest enemy (725 strength) and we should capitalize on this opportunity.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 55
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Further strengthening relationship with Polynesia (Public 52→55, Private 38→40). With India attacking The Netherlands, Polynesia becomes more important as a counterweight. Polynesia has 14 delegates, Order ideology like India (may have diplomatic influence), and is at war with Persia (our enemy). Stronger ties may encourage them to pressure India diplomatically or support The Netherlands in World Congress, given India's aggressive behavior threatens the entire alliance network.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### India
        - Public: -45
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: India declared war on The Netherlands, triggering America's defensive pact obligation. Significantly worsening relationship from -35/-25 to -45/-30 as this is now an active war threatening our strategic ally network. India's military (19,745) is 4.5x America's (4,419), making them an existential threat. The stronger hostility signal guides AI to treat India as primary threat and coordinate defensive operations with The Netherlands. Maintaining some restraint (not -100/-100) to avoid India focusing all aggression solely on America given the power disparity and our need for preparation time. This escalation reflects the dire strategic reality created by India's declaration of war on our ally.
        - UpdatedTurn: 355
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: 45
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Strengthening alliance with The Netherlands from 40/40 to 45/45 after India's declaration of war. They are now under direct attack from our greatest threat, and our defensive pact makes this a shared conflict. The Netherlands has 23 World Congress delegates (51% victory progress) - their survival and cooperation is critical for diplomatic victory prospects and balancing India's power. Stronger support signals solidarity and may enable coordinated defense against India's overwhelming forces. Positive relationship maintained or slightly enhanced to ensure they view America as reliable ally during this crisis and don't seek separate peace that would leave America isolated facing India's military.
        - UpdatedTurn: 355
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Adjusting flavors for multi-front war against superior enemies. Nuke increased to 85 from 90 (we have 0 Uranium, still prioritized but realistic). UseNuke increased to 75 from 60 as nuclear weapons are now legitimate deterrents given India's aggression. Antiair maxed at 100 from 95 (air defense is critical given India's air power and our vulnerability). Air increased to 90 from 95 (sstill high but slightly lower than antiair priority). Offense reduced to 35 from 40 (defensive posture against all fronts). Defense/CityDefense/MilitaryTraining remain maxed - survival depends on strong defensive units and garrisons. Production reduced to 90 from 95 (happiness matters more in multiple wars). Science remains 100 (tech is survival). Gold increased to 80 from 85 (need for unit purchases/upgrades and uranium diplomacy). Diplomacy increased to 90 from 85 (critical for securing uranium and coordinating with allies Netherlands, Polynesia, Egypt). Happiness increased to 80 from 75 (combat penalty from unhappiness is devastating in multiple wars). Espionage increased to 90 from 85 (need intel on India's movements and nuclear capabilities).
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
        
        ### Flavors
        - Offense: 35
        - Defense: 100
        - Mobilization: 95
        - CityDefense: 100
        - MilitaryTraining: 100
        - Recon: 50

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 8 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Persia:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 331
          - Open Door: Wittenberg:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Panama City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Sigtuna:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 291
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Ban Chiang:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 353
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 353
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: Dutch Sphere of Influence: Jakarta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

        You, Washington (leader of America, Player 4), are making strategic decisions after turn 356.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Washington, leader of America (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: America
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Minuteman, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Monitor, Replacing Ironclad (Requires technology: Industrialization)
        - Unique Building: Independence Hall, Replacing Royal Collection (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Building: Homestead, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Civilization Ability: Military Land Units start with the Sentry Promotion. Tile purchase cost is reduced by 25% and is reset upon founding a City. +20 Production when purchasing tiles, scaling with Era. Can purchase tiles owned by others.
      - Leader: Washington
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Setting next policy to 'New World Order' (Level 1 Autocracy Tenet). Our empire is suffering from severe unhappiness (39% happiness), which incurs devastating combat penalties across all active war fronts. New World Order provides -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all cities, which will immediately restore happiness and eliminate combat penalties. Additionally, it boosts culture and production from Constabularies and Police Stations, which we have constructed across our empire, helping us accelerate future policies and military production. This is a critical strategic pivot to resolve our most pressing domestic bottleneck.
        - Policy: New World Order
    - #1:

      - args:
        - Technology: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: Selecting 'Nuclear Fission' as our next technology. We have completed the Manhattan Project and our workers have successfully constructed a quarry on our newly revealed Uranium resource. Nuclear Fission will unlock Atomic Bombs and Nuclear Plants, providing us with the ultimate strategic deterrent against the overwhelmingly superior military of India and the aggressive forces of Poland. This is the cornerstone of our survival and spaceship victory strategy.

Tile Improvement and Infrastructure are increased to 55 and 75 respectively to prioritize railroad connections and working our newly secured Uranium resource. Antiair, Defense, and City Defense remain maxed out at 100 to safeguard our home cities against Poland's air force and landships while we finish off Persia and construct our strategic deterrent.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Advancing Technological Horizon**
            
            My focus is now on the immediate implications of researching Rocketry. I'm evaluating potential next steps in scientific advancement and considering how this breakthrough can be leveraged strategically within the current game state.
            
            **Selecting Nuclear Fission**
            
            I've confirmed that "Nuclear Fission" is indeed an available technology choice, which aligns with our strategic goal for deterrence. Interestingly, while a quarry was built on a Uranium tile, current resource checks indicate no available Uranium. This warrants further investigation into resource acquisition and processing.
            
            **Confirming Uranium Access**
            
            I'm confirming that the Quarry on the Uranium tile should yield resources imminently, despite the current zero count, as it was completed this turn and Uranium should be revealed by Atomic Theory, which we researched for Rocketry. With this assurance, I'm prioritizing Nuclear Fission as our next critical research goal.
            
            **Evaluating Policy Options**
            
            My attention has shifted to policy choices. The initial plan to adopt "Elite Forces" remains, but I'm reviewing all available Autocracy, Statecraft, Artistry, and newly unlocked policies like Imperialism and Industry to ensure the optimal strategic alignment given our current ongoing conflicts.
            
            **Investigating Alliance Trigger**
            
            I'm currently focused on understanding why our defensive pact with the Netherlands hasn't activated during their war with India. I need to determine the precise conditions for pact activation and explore if any existing relationships or game mechanics are preventing the alliance from engaging.
            
            **Analyzing Alliance Activation**
            
            I've determined the defensive pact with the Netherlands didn't trigger because they were the aggressor. Crucially, we are not at war with India, whose military strength is significantly higher than ours. Our current zones show overwhelming dominance against Persia, with minimal enemy presence remaining.
            
            **Crushing Persian Opposition**
            
            Persia is nearly defeated with a military strength of 452, allowing for imminent vassalization or city capture. However, a grave threat emerges from Poland; our war score is negative, and their forces, including bombers and machine guns, dominate key zones near their cities, Bydgoszcz and Lodz. They've also captured San Francisco.
            
            **Defeating Polish Naval Threat**
            
            I'm pleased to report that my analysis of recent combat logs has uncovered a significant success: we successfully destroyed a Polish Dreadnought in a naval engagement near the Ocean at (20, 20). This was a crucial victory, especially given Poland's current military dominance and their capture of San Francisco.
            
            **Defending Northern Waters**
            
            I'm actively engaging Poland's naval forces in the northern seas, near Chicago and Seattle, and have successfully sunk one of their Dreadnoughts. However, India is significantly ahead in technology and military strength, posing a considerable challenge to our science victory goals.
            
            **Assessing Cultural Threats**
            
            I'm now re-evaluating potential victory conditions, specifically the looming threat of a Cultural Victory from India. Their influence is widespread, and with only a few more civilizations to sway, they could win soon. The World Congress votes are also drawing near, and I need to assess our diplomatic standing.
            
            **Revising Portugal Strategy**
            
            I'm reassessing my approach to Portugal following their denouncement due to my Autocracy choice and past territorial actions. Their alliances with Poland and Polynesia, along with a DoF with Egypt, present a complex geopolitical situation requiring careful consideration and a potential shift in my diplomatic stance.
            
            **Maintaining Diplomatic Peace**
            
            I've decided against escalating our relationship with Portugal to hostile. Provoking them could trigger a disastrous multi-front war, pulling in Polynesia against us while we're already engaged with Poland and India. I will maintain a neutral or slightly negative stance to prevent AI-initiated aggression.
            
            **Addressing Unhappiness Impact**
            
            My immediate concern is the significant unhappiness, evidenced by a 39% happiness percentage, which is directly impacting combat effectiveness. This situation demands urgent attention to implement policies or actions that will improve morale and bolster our military performance against Poland and to avoid further losses.
            
            **Addressing Unhappiness Bottleneck**
            
            I'm pinpointing the cause of widespread unhappiness across my cities, largely attributing it to war weariness from ongoing conflicts. My focus is now on prioritizing actions that will boost happiness and culture, as this negative morale is severely hampering combat and overall progress.
            
            **Adopting New World Order**
            
            I've identified "New World Order" as a crucial policy choice to directly address our widespread unhappiness. This tenet offers a direct reduction in distress and enhances our culture generation, which will be vital for combating India's looming cultural victory.
            
            **Revising Policy Choice**
            
            I've realized "New World Order" is a far superior policy to "Elite Forces" given our current situation. The happiness and culture boosts it provides will be critical for countering India's cultural victory and improving our combat effectiveness against Poland, far outweighing any defensive experience benefits.
            
            **Prioritizing Happiness Benefits**
            
            I've re-evaluated the policy choices and am now prioritizing "New World Order" over "Elite Forces". The substantial happiness and culture boosts it offers are far more critical for addressing our current combat penalties and countering India's cultural victory than any XP gains.
            
            **Confirming Policy and Technology**
            
            I've confirmed "New World Order" is available and is my current policy focus. My previous research on Rocketry is complete, and I'm now verifying the Uranium resource availability after quarry construction, paving the way for researching "Nuclear Fission" to unlock atomic capabilities.
            
            **Securing Nuclear Deterrence**
            
            I've confirmed we completed the Manhattan Project previously, and with Uranium access imminent and "Nuclear Fission" being my next technology, nuclear deterrence is within immediate reach. This is a critical development for deterring both Poland and India.
            
            **Investigating Alliance Dynamics**
            
            I'm delving into the intricacies of our defensive pact with the Netherlands and why it failed to activate during their conflict with India. My current hypothesis centers on the Netherlands initiating the aggression, thereby nullifying the defensive clause. I'm also examining the potential influence of our ongoing war with Poland and Persia on alliance mechanics, as well as the cascading effects of Egypt's vassalage under India and the Netherlands' subsequent war with Egypt.
            
            **Analyzing Global Conflicts**
            
            I am assessing the intricate web of ongoing wars across the globe, noting Persia's widespread conflicts. My current focus is on our relationships with key players like Poland, India, and Persia, aiming to maintain strategic positions while avoiding unnecessary escalation, especially with India.
            
            **Monitoring Alliance Activations**
            
            I'm closely monitoring alliance dynamics, specifically the defensive pact with the Netherlands. It seems their war with India, potentially involving Vilnius, is unfolding, and I need to ensure our own relationships with allies like Polynesia and Egypt remain stable amidst these shifting global conflicts.
            
            **Clarifying Valletta Relationships**
            
            I'm correcting my understanding of Valletta's status. Previous assumptions about their declarations of war against us and our allies were mistaken; current reports show them as friends with us and at peace with everyone. This significantly alters my strategic outlook regarding potential city-state interactions and their impact on global alliances.
            
            **Revising City-State Diplomacy**
            
            I've realized my attempts to adjust city-state relationships using `set-relationship` are futile as the command only applies to major civilizations. This necessitates a strategic re-evaluation of how I influence Valletta, as direct diplomatic modifiers are not an option.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Parameters**
            
            I've confirmed that the `set-relationship` command only impacts major civilizations, not city-states like Valletta. My focus is now on optimizing relationships with our current major allies and adversaries, ensuring these parameters are stable and strategically advantageous for ongoing conflicts and potential diplomatic shifts.
            
            **Optimizing AI Flavors**
            
            I've reviewed the current AI flavor settings and found them to be quite balanced for our defensive and diplomatic stance. My current focus is on adjusting these flavors, alongside my technology and policy research, to best achieve our immediate goals: rapid research of Nuclear Fission, defending against Poland, finishing off Persia, and resolving our unhappiness.
            
            **Adjusting AI Priorities**
            
            I am now focusing on tweaking AI flavors. I've increased the priority for Happiness to 95-100 and Culture to 75, as the AI needs to address my current severe unhappiness penalty and counter India's cultural victory. Other flavors like Defense and Science remain high to support our immediate strategic needs.
            
            **Refining AI Preferences**
            
            I'm now fine-tuning the AI's flavor settings to address critical needs. I've boosted Happiness to 95-100 and Culture to 75 to counter severe unhappiness and India's cultural victory. Science, Defense, and related military flavors remain at 100 to support our immediate strategic priorities.
            
            **Setting Next Policy**
            
            My next policy choice is confirmed as "New World Order." This tenet directly addresses our critical unhappiness bottleneck, which is currently imposing severe combat penalties. It will also bolster culture, crucial for countering India's looming victory.
            
            **Adopting Crucial Policy**
            
            I'm implementing "New World Order" to address severe unhappiness, a critical bottleneck. This policy will immediately restore happiness, eliminate combat penalties, and boost culture and production, vital for countering India's cultural victory and accelerating future endeavors.
            
            **Selecting Next Technology**
            
            My next technology choice is confirmed: "Nuclear Fission." With the Manhattan Project complete and Uranium secured, this will unlock atomic deterrents against Poland and India, crucial for survival and our spaceship victory strategy.
            
            **Confirming Final Decisions**
            
            I've confirmed that "New World Order" is the optimal policy choice due to its significant happiness and culture boosts. My focus will now be on executing `set-policy`, `set-research`, and `set-flavors` to implement these strategic decisions effectively.
            
            **Implementing Strategic Shifts**
            
            I am implementing the crucial policy "New World Order" to combat severe unhappiness and boost culture, alongside researching "Nuclear Fission" for atomic deterrence. My focus is on optimizing AI flavors to support these critical objectives.

            - Rationale: Selecting 'Nuclear Fission' as our next technology. We have completed the Manhattan Project and our workers have successfully constructed a quarry on our newly revealed Uranium resource. Nuclear Fission will unlock Atomic Bombs and Nuclear Plants, providing us with the ultimate strategic deterrent against the overwhelmingly superior military of India and the aggressive forces of Poland. This is the cornerstone of our survival and spaceship victory strategy.
